a^{\prime}\in\xi_{i}
.
Moreover, by hypothesis
b_{i}\{x:=a^{\prime}\}\in\xi^{\prime}

and, by compatibility of
\to_{\mathbf{U}}
-reduction under substitution,

b_{i}\{x:=a^{\prime}\}\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}b^{\prime}_{i}\{x:=a^{\prime}\}\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}c
.
Since
\xi^{\prime}
is closed by reduction, this implies that

c\in\xi^{\prime}
, as required.
∎

Lemma 37

(Adequacy of abstraction)
.

Let
\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\in\mathbf{RC}
.
Let
a
be such that for every
b\in\xi_{1}

we have that
a\{x:=b\}\in\xi_{2}
.
Then
\lambda_{x}.\,a\in\xi_{1}\bm{\rightarrow}\xi_{2}
.

Proof.

Note that
x\in\xi_{1}
,
so by hypothesis
a=a\{x:=x\}\in\xi_{2}
.
In particular,
a\in\mathbf{SN}
.
From this it is immediate to conclude that
\lambda_{x}.\,a\in\mathbf{SN}
.
To see that
\lambda_{x}.\,a\in\xi_{1}\bm{\rightarrow}\xi_{2}
,
by definition of the arrow operator, let
b\in\xi_{1}
and let us
show that
(\lambda_{x}.\,a)@b\in\xi_{2}
.

First we claim that
(\lambda_{x}.\,a)@b\in\mathbf{SN}
.
We have already argued that
\lambda_{x}.\,a\in\mathbf{SN}
,
and moreover
b\in\mathbf{SN}
since
b\in\xi_{1}
.
By induction on
\#(\lambda_{x}.\,a)+\#(b)
,
we argue that if
(\lambda_{x}.\,a)@b\to_{\mathbf{U}}c

then
c\in\mathbf{SN}
.
We consider three cases, depending on whether the reduction step
is internal to
\lambda_{x}.\,a
,
internal to
b
,
or at the root:

1.

If the reduction step is internal to
\lambda_{x}.\,a
,
that is, the step is of the form

(\lambda_{x}.\,a)@b\to_{\mathbf{U}}(\lambda_{x}.\,a^{\prime})@b

with
\lambda_{x}.\,a\to_{\mathbf{U}}\lambda_{x}.\,a^{\prime}
,
then
\#(\lambda_{x}.\,a)+\#(b)>\#(\lambda_{x}.\,a^{\prime})+\#(b)
.
Note that
a^{\prime}
still has the property that
for every
c\in\xi_{1}

we have that
a^{\prime}\{x:=c\}\in\xi^{\prime}
,
because
\xi^{\prime}
is closed by reduction,
and
a\{x:=c\}\to_{\mathbf{U}}a^{\prime}\{x:=c\}
,
and furthermore
a\{x:=c\}\in\xi^{\prime}

holds by hypothesis.
Hence, by IH, we have that
(\lambda_{x}.\,a^{\prime})@b\in\mathbf{SN}
.

2.

If the reduction step is internal to
b
,
that is, the step is of the form

(\lambda_{x}.\,a)@b\to_{\mathbf{U}}(\lambda_{x}.\,a)@b^{\prime}

with
b\to_{\mathbf{U}}b^{\prime}
,
then
\#(\lambda_{x}.\,a)+\#(b)>\#(\lambda_{x}.\,a)+\#(b^{\prime})
.
Note that
b^{\prime}\in\xi_{1}
still holds
because
\xi_{1}
is closed by reduction.
Hence, by IH, we have that
(\lambda_{x}.\,a)@b^{\prime}\in\mathbf{SN}
.

3.

If the reduction step is at the root,
then the step must be of the form

(\lambda_{x}.\,a)@b\to_{\mathbf{U}}a\{x:=b\}
.
Thus, by hypothesis,
a\{x:=b\}\in\xi_{2}
,
which implies that
a\{x:=b\}\in\mathbf{SN}
.

Second, to see that
(\lambda_{x}.\,a)@b\in\xi^{\prime}
,
since
\xi^{\prime}
is complete, it suffices to show that all canonical
reducts of
(\lambda_{x}.\,a)@b
are in
\xi^{\prime}
.
That is, let
(\lambda_{x}.\,a)@b\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}c\in\mathbf{CAN}

and let us show that
c\in\xi^{\prime}
.
Note that the reduction sequence

(\lambda_{x}.\,a)@b\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}c

must be of the form

(\lambda_{x}.\,a)@b\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}(\lambda_{x}.\,a^{\prime})@b^{\prime}\to_{\mathbf{U}}a^{\prime}\{x:=b^{\prime}\}\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}c

with
a\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}a^{\prime}
and
b\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}b^{\prime}
.
Note that
b^{\prime}\in\xi_{1}
since
\xi_{1}
is closed by reduction.
Hence by hypothesis
a\{x:=b^{\prime}\}\in\xi^{\prime}
.
Moreover, by compatibility of
\to_{\mathbf{U}}
-reduction under substitution,

a\{x:=b^{\prime}\}\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}a\{x:=b^{\prime}\}\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}c
.
Since
\xi^{\prime}
is closed by reduction, this implies that
c\in\xi^{\prime}
,
as required.
∎

Lemma 38

(Adequacy of negation introduction)
.

Let
\xi\in\mathbf{RC}
.
If
a\in\xi
then
\mathsf{N}a\in\bm{\mathord{\sim}}\xi
.

Proof.

First note that
a\in\mathbf{SN}
because
a\in\xi
.
From this it is immediate to conclude that
\mathsf{N}a\in\mathbf{SN}
.
Second, to see that
\mathsf{N}a\in\bm{\mathord{\sim}}\xi
, by definition of
the negation operator for r.c.’s,
it suffices to show that all canonical reducts of
\mathsf{N}a

are of the form
\mathsf{N}a^{\prime}
with
a^{\prime}\in\xi
.
Indeed, let
\mathsf{N}a\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}c\in\mathbf{CAN}
,
and note that the reduction must be of the form

\mathsf{N}a\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}\mathsf{N}a^{\prime}
with
a\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}a^{\prime}
.
Since
\xi
is closed by reduction, we have that
a^{\prime}\in\xi
,
as required.
∎

Lemma 39

(Adequacy of co-implication elimination)
.

Let
\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi^{\prime}\in\mathbf{RC}
.
Let
a\in\xi_{1}\bm{\ltimes}\xi_{2}
,
and let
b
be a term such that
for all
a_{1}\in\xi_{1}
and for all
a_{2}\in\xi_{2}

we have
b\{x:=a_{1}\}\{y:=a_{2}\}\in\xi^{\prime}
.
Then
\varrho{a}[_{x;y}.{b}]\in\xi^{\prime}
.

Proof.

First we claim that
\varrho{a}[_{x;y}.{b}]\in\mathbf{SN}
.
Note that
a\in\mathbf{SN}
since
a\in\xi_{1}\bm{\ltimes}\xi_{2}
.
Moreover,
b\in\mathbf{SN}

because
x\in\xi_{1}
and
y\in\xi_{2}
,
so
b=b\{x:=x\}\{y:=y\}\in\xi^{\prime}
.
By induction on
\#(a)+\#(b)
,
we argue that if
\varrho{a}[_{x;y}.{b}]\to_{\mathbf{U}}c

then
c\in\mathbf{SN}
.
We consider three cases, depending on whether the reduction step is internal
to
a
, internal to
b
, or at the root:

1.

If the reduction step is internal to
a
,
that is, the step is of the form

\varrho{a}[_{x;y}.{b}]\to_{\mathbf{U}}\varrho{a^{\prime}}[_{x;y}.{b}]

with
a\to_{\mathbf{U}}a^{\prime}
,
then
\#(a)+\#(b)>\#(a^{\prime})+\#(b)
.
Note that
a^{\prime}\in\xi_{1}\bm{\ltimes}\xi_{2}
still holds
since
\xi_{1}\bm{\ltimes}\xi_{2}
is closed by reduction.
Hence, by IH, we have that

\varrho{a^{\prime}}[_{x;y}.{b}]\in\mathbf{SN}
.

2.

If the reduction step is internal to
b
,
that is, the step is of the form

\varrho{a}[_{x;y}.{b}]\to_{\mathbf{U}}\varrho{a}[_{x;y}.{b^{\prime}}]

with
b\to_{\mathbf{U}}b^{\prime}
,
then
\#(a)+\#(b)>\#(a)+\#(b^{\prime})
.
Note that
b^{\prime}
still has the property that
for every
a_{1}\in\xi_{1}
and every
a_{2}\in\xi_{2}

we have
b^{\prime}\{x:=a_{1}\}\{y:=a_{2}\}\in\xi^{\prime}
,
because

b\{x:=a_{1}\}\{y:=a_{2}\}\to_{\mathbf{U}}b^{\prime}\{x:=a_{1}\}\{y:=a_{2}\}

and
\xi^{\prime}
is closed by reduction
and furthermore
b\{x:=a_{1}\}\{y:=a_{2}\}\in\xi^{\prime}

holds by hypothesis.
Hence, by IH, we have that

\varrho{a}[_{x;y}.{b^{\prime}}]\in\mathbf{SN}
.

3.

If the reduction step is at the root, then the step must be of the form

\varrho{(a_{1}\,\bm{;}a_{2})}[_{x;y}.{b}]\to b\{x:=a_{1}\}\{y:=a_{2}\}
,
where
a=(a_{1}\,\bm{;}a_{2})
.
Since
a=(a_{1}\,\bm{;}a_{2})
is canonical
and
(a_{1}\,\bm{;}a_{2})\in\xi_{1}\bm{\ltimes}\xi_{2}
,
by definition of the co-implication operator on reducibility candidates,
we have that
a_{1}\in\xi_{1}
and
a_{2}\in\xi_{2}
.
Thus, by hypothesis,

b\{x:=a_{1}\}\{y:=a_{2}\}\in\xi^{\prime}
,
which implies

b\{x:=a_{1}\}\{y:=a_{2}\}\in\mathbf{SN}
.

Second, to see that
\varrho{a}[_{x;y}.{b}]\in\xi^{\prime}
,
since
\xi^{\prime}
is complete, it suffices to show that all canonical reducts
of
\varrho{a}[_{x;y}.{b}]
are in
\xi^{\prime}
.
That is, let
\varrho{a}[_{x;y}.{b}]\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}c\in\mathbf{CAN}

and let us show that
c\in\xi^{\prime}
.
Note that the reduction sequence

\varrho{a}[_{x;y}.{b}]\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}c

must be of the form

\varrho{a}[_{x;y}.{b}]\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}\varrho{(a_{1}\,\bm{;}a_{2})}[_{x;y}.{b^{\prime}}]\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}b^{\prime}\{x:=a_{1}\}\{y:=a_{2}\}\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}c

where
a\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}(a_{1}\,\bm{;}a_{2})

and
b\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}b^{\prime}
.
Since
(a_{1}\,\bm{;}a_{2})

is a canonical reduct of
a\in\xi_{1}\bm{\ltimes}\xi_{2}
,
by definition of the co-implication operator on reducibility candidates,
we have that
a_{1}\in\xi_{1}
and
a_{2}\in\xi_{2}
.
Moreover, by hypothesis

b\{x:=a_{1}\}\{y:=a_{2}\}\in\xi^{\prime}
and,
by compatibility of
\to_{\mathbf{U}}
-reduction under substitution,

b\{x:=a_{1}\}\{y:=a_{2}\}\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}b^{\prime}\{x:=a_{1}\}\{y:=a_{2}\}\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}c
.
Since
\xi^{\prime}
is closed by reduction, this implies that
c\in\xi^{\prime}
,
as required.
∎

Lemma 40

(Adequacy of negation elimination)
.

Let
\xi\in\mathbf{RC}
.
If
a\in\bm{\mathord{\sim}}\xi
then
\mathsf{M}a\in\xi
.

Proof.

First we claim that
\mathsf{M}a\in\mathbf{SN}
.
Note that
a\in\mathbf{SN}
since
a\in\bm{\mathord{\sim}}\xi
.
By induction on
\#(a)
, we argue that if

\mathsf{M}a\to_{\mathbf{U}}c
then
c\in\mathbf{SN}
.
We consider two cases, depending on whether the reduction step
is internal to
a
or at the root:

1.

If the reduction step is internal to
a
,
that is,
\mathsf{M}a\to_{\mathbf{U}}\mathsf{M}a^{\prime}
with
a\to_{\mathbf{U}}a^{\prime}
,
then
\#(a)>\#(a^{\prime})
.
Note that
a^{\prime}\in\bm{\mathord{\sim}}\xi
still holds since
\bm{\mathord{\sim}}\xi

is closed by reduction. Hence, by IH, we have that

\mathsf{M}a^{\prime}\in\mathbf{SN}
.

2.

If the reduction step is at the root, then the step must be of the
form
\mathsf{M}(\mathsf{N}a^{\prime})\to_{\mathbf{U}}a^{\prime}

where
a=\mathsf{N}a^{\prime}
.
Since
a=\mathsf{N}a^{\prime}
is canonical and
\mathsf{N}a^{\prime}\in\bm{\mathord{\sim}}\xi
,
by definition of the negation operator for r.c.’s,
we have that
a^{\prime}\in\xi
,
which implies
a^{\prime}\in\mathbf{SN}
.

Second, to see that
\mathsf{M}a\in\xi
, since
\xi
is complete,
it suffices to show that all canonical reducts of
\mathsf{M}a

are in
\xi
. That is, let
\mathsf{M}a\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}c\in\mathbf{CAN}

and let us show that
c\in\xi
.
Note that the reduction sequence
\mathsf{M}a\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}c

must be of the form

\mathsf{M}a\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}\mathsf{M}(\mathsf{N}a^{\prime})\to_{\mathbf{U}}a^{\prime}\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}c

with
a\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}\mathsf{N}a^{\prime}
.
Since
\mathsf{N}a^{\prime}
is a canonical reduct of
a\in\bm{\mathord{\sim}}\xi
,
by definition of the negation operator for r.c.’s,
we have that
a^{\prime}\in\xi
.
Since
\xi
is closed by reduction, this implies that
c\in\xi
,
as required.
∎

Lemma 41

(Adequacy of universal abstraction)
.

Suppose that
\{\xi_{i}\}_{i\in{I}}\subseteq\mathbf{RC}
,
where
I
is assumed to be non-empty.
If
a\in\xi_{i}
for all
i\in{I}
,
then
\lambda_{\Diamond}.\,a\in\bm{\Pi}_{i\in{I}}\,{\xi_{i}}
.

Proof.

Since
I
is non-empty,
a\in\xi_{i_{0}}

for at least one index
i_{0}\in I
.
This implies that
a\in\mathbf{SN}
, from which it is immediate to
conclude that
\lambda_{\Diamond}.\,a\in\mathbf{SN}
.
To see that
\lambda_{\Diamond}.\,a\in\bm{\Pi}_{i\in{I}}\,{\xi_{i}}
,
by definition of the indexed product,
let
j\in I
be an arbitrary index
and let us show that
(\lambda_{\Diamond}.\,a)@\Diamond\in\xi_{j}
.

First, we claim that
(\lambda_{\Diamond}.\,a)@\Diamond\in\mathbf{SN}
.
By induction on
\#(\lambda_{\Diamond}.\,a)
, we argue that if

(\lambda_{\Diamond}.\,a)@\Diamond\to_{\mathbf{U}}c
then
c\in\mathbf{SN}
.
We consider two cases, depending on whether the reduction step is
internal to
\lambda_{\Diamond}.\,a
or at the root:

1.

If the reduction step is internal to
\lambda_{\Diamond}.\,a
,
that is, the step is of the form

(\lambda_{\Diamond}.\,a)@\Diamond\to_{\mathbf{U}}(\lambda_{\Diamond}.\,a^{\prime})@\Diamond

with
\lambda_{\Diamond}.\,a\to_{\mathbf{U}}\lambda_{\Diamond}.\,a^{\prime}
,
then
\#(\lambda_{\Diamond}.\,a)>\#(\lambda_{\Diamond}.\,a^{\prime})
.
Note that
a^{\prime}\in\xi_{j}
,
because
\xi_{j}
is closed by reduction,
and
a\to_{\mathbf{U}}a^{\prime}
,
and furthermore
a\in\xi_{j}
holds by hypothesis.
Hence by IH we have that
(\lambda_{\Diamond}.\,a)@\Diamond\in\mathbf{SN}
.

2.

If the reduction step is at the root, then the step must be
of the form
(\lambda_{\Diamond}.\,a)@\Diamond\to_{\mathbf{U}}a
.
By hypothesis,
a\in\xi_{j}
, which implies that
a\in\mathbf{SN}
.

Second, to see that

(\lambda_{\Diamond}.\,a)@\Diamond\in\xi_{j}
, since
\xi_{j}
is complete,
it suffices to show that all canonical reducts of

(\lambda_{\Diamond}.\,a)@\Diamond
are in
\xi_{j}
.
That is, let
(\lambda_{\Diamond}.\,a)@\Diamond\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}c\in\mathbf{CAN}
,
and let us show that
c\in\xi_{j}
.
Note that the reduction sequence

(\lambda_{\Diamond}.\,a)@\Diamond\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}c

must be of the form

(\lambda_{\Diamond}.\,a)@\Diamond\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}(\lambda_{\Diamond}.\,a^{\prime})@\Diamond\to_{\mathbf{U}}a^{\prime}\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}c

with
a\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}a^{\prime}
.
Since
\xi_{j}
is closed by reduction,
and
a\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}a^{\prime}\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}c
,
and furthermore
a\in\xi_{j}
,
we conclude that
c\in\xi_{j}
,
as required.
∎

Lemma 42

(Adequacy of existential introduction)
.

Let
\{\xi_{i}\}_{i\in{I}}\subseteq\mathbf{RC}
.
If
a\in\xi_{j}
for some
j\in I
,
then
\langle\Diamond,a\rangle\in\bm{\Sigma}_{i\in{I}}\,{\xi_{i}}
.

Proof.

First note that
a\in\mathbf{SN}
because
a\in\xi_{j}
.
From this it is immediate to conclude that
\langle\Diamond,a\rangle\in\mathbf{SN}
.
Second, to see that
\langle\Diamond,a\rangle\in\bm{\Sigma}_{i\in{I}}\,{\xi_{i}}
, by
definition of the indexed sum, it suffices to show that all canonical
reducts of
\langle\Diamond,a\rangle
are of the form
\langle\Diamond,a^{\prime}\rangle
with

a^{\prime}\in\xi_{i}
for some
i\in{I}
.
More specifically, we argue that, in this case,
a^{\prime}\in\xi_{j}
.
Indeed, let
\langle\Diamond,a\rangle\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}c\in\mathbf{CAN}

and note that the reduction must be of the form

\langle\Diamond,a\rangle\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}\langle\Diamond,a^{\prime}\rangle
with
a\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}a^{\prime}
.
Since
\xi_{j}
is closed by reduction, we have that
a^{\prime}\in\xi_{j}
,
as required.
∎

Lemma 43

(Adequacy of existential elimination)
.

Suppose that
\{\xi_{i}\}_{i\in{I}}\subseteq\mathbf{RC}
,
where
I
is assumed to be non-empty,
and let
\xi^{\prime}\in\mathbf{RC}
.
Let
a\in\bm{\Sigma}_{i\in{I}}\,{\xi_{i}}
,
and let
b
be such that
for all
i\in{I}
and for all
a^{\prime}\in\xi_{i}

we have that
b\{x:=a^{\prime}\}\in\xi^{\prime}
.
Then
\nabla\,a[_{(\Diamond,x)}.b]\in\xi^{\prime}
.

Proof.

First we claim that
\nabla\,a[_{(\Diamond,x)}.b]\in\mathbf{SN}
.
Note that
a\in\mathbf{SN}
because
a\in\bm{\Sigma}_{i\in{I}}\,{\xi_{i}}
.
Moreover, since
I
is non-empty, there is at least one index
i_{0}\in I
,
and
x\in\xi_{i_{0}}
, so by hypothesis

b=b\{x:=x\}\in\xi^{\prime}
, which implies that

b\in\mathbf{SN}
.
By induction on
\#(a)+\#(b)
,
we argue that if
\nabla\,a[_{(\Diamond,x)}.b]\to_{\mathbf{U}}c

then
c\in\mathbf{SN}
.
We consider three cases, depending on whether the reduction
step is internal to
a
, internal to
b
, or at the root:

1.

If the reduction step is internal to
a
,
that is, the step is of the form

\nabla\,a[_{(\Diamond,x)}.b]\to_{\mathbf{U}}\nabla\,a^{\prime}[_{(\Diamond,x)}.b]

with
a\to_{\mathbf{U}}a^{\prime}
,
then
\#(a)+\#(b)>\#(a^{\prime})+\#(b)
.
Note that
a^{\prime}\in\bm{\Sigma}_{i\in{I}}\,{\xi_{i}}

still holds since
\bm{\Sigma}_{i\in{I}}\,{\xi_{i}}
is closed by reduction.
Hence, by IH, we have that

\nabla\,a^{\prime}[_{(\Diamond,x)}.b]\in\mathbf{SN}
.

2.

If the reduction step is internal to
b
,
that is, the step is of the form

\nabla\,a[_{(\Diamond,x)}.b]\to_{\mathbf{U}}\nabla\,a[_{(\Diamond,x)}.b^{\prime}]

with
b\to_{\mathbf{U}}b^{\prime}
,
then
\#(a)+\#(b)>\#(a)+\#(b^{\prime})
.
Note that
b^{\prime}
still has the property that
for all
i\in{I}
and all
a^{\prime}\in\xi_{i}

we have
b^{\prime}\{x:=a^{\prime}\}\in\xi^{\prime}
,
because
b\{x:=a^{\prime}\}\to_{\mathbf{U}}b^{\prime}\{x:=a^{\prime}\}

and
\xi^{\prime}
is closed by reduction,
and furthermore
b\{x:=a^{\prime}\}\in\xi^{\prime}

holds by hypothesis.
Hence, by IH, we have that

\nabla\,a[_{(\Diamond,x)}.b^{\prime}]\in\mathbf{SN}
.

3.

If the reduction step is at the root, then the step must be of the form

\nabla\,\langle\Diamond,a^{\prime}\rangle[_{(\Diamond,x)}.b]\to_{\mathbf{U}}b\{x:=a^{\prime}\}

where
a=\langle\Diamond,a^{\prime}\rangle
.
Since
a=\langle\Diamond,a^{\prime}\rangle
is canonical and

\langle\Diamond,a^{\prime}\rangle\in\bm{\Sigma}_{i\in{I}}\,{\xi_{i}}
,
by definition of the indexed sum,
we have that
a^{\prime}\in\xi_{j}
for some
j\in{I}
.
Thus, by hypothesis,
b\{x:=a^{\prime}\}\in\xi^{\prime}
,
which implies that
b\{x:=a^{\prime}\}\in\mathbf{SN}
.

Second, to see that
\nabla\,a[_{(\Diamond,x)}.b]\in\xi^{\prime}
,
since
\xi^{\prime}
is complete, it suffices to show that all canonical
reducts of
\nabla\,a[_{(\Diamond,x)}.b]
are in
\xi^{\prime}
.
That is, let
\nabla\,a[_{(\Diamond,x)}.b]\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}c\in\mathbf{CAN}

and let us show that
c\in\xi^{\prime}
.
Note that the reduction sequence

\nabla\,a[_{(\Diamond,x)}.b]\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}c

must be of the form

\nabla\,a[_{(\Diamond,x)}.b]\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}\nabla\,\langle\Diamond,a^{\prime}\rangle[_{(\Diamond,x)}.b^{\prime}]\to_{\mathbf{U}}b^{\prime}\{x:=a^{\prime}\}\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}c
.
where
a\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}\langle\Diamond,a^{\prime}\rangle
and
b\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}b^{\prime}
.
Since
\langle\Diamond,a^{\prime}\rangle
is a canonical reduct of
a\in\bm{\Sigma}_{i\in{I}}\,{\xi_{i}}
,
by definition of the indexed sum, we have that there exists an
index
j\in{I}
such that
a^{\prime}\in\xi_{j}
.
Moreover, by hypothesis,
b\{x:=a^{\prime}\}\in\xi^{\prime}

and, by compatibility of
\to_{\mathbf{U}}
-reduction under substitution,

b\{x:=a^{\prime}\}\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}b^{\prime}\{x:=a^{\prime}\}\to^{*}_{\mathbf{U}}c
.
Since
\xi^{\prime}
is closed by reduction, this implies that

c\in\xi^{\prime}
, as required.
∎

Definition 44

(Adequate substitutions)
.

A substitution is a function
\sigma
mapping
each variable to an term in
\mathbf{U}
.
We write
\sigma[x:=a]
for the substitution
\sigma^{\prime}

that results from extending
\sigma
in such a way that

\sigma^{\prime}(x)=a
and
\sigma^{\prime}(y)=\sigma(y)

for any other variable
y\neq x
.
We write
a^{\sigma}
for the term that results from
the capture-avoiding substitution
of each free occurrence of each variable
x
in
a

by
\sigma(x)
.
We say that the substitution
\sigma
is
adequate for the typing context
\Gamma
under the environment
\rho
,
and we write
\sigma\vDash_{\rho}\Gamma
,
if for each type assignment
(x:P)\in\Gamma

we have that
\sigma(x)\in[\![P]\!]_{\rho}
.

Theorem 45

(Adequacy)
.

If
\Gamma\vdash t:P

and
\sigma\vDash_{\rho}\Gamma

then
|t|^{\sigma}\in[\![P]\!]_{\rho}
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction
on the derivation of the typing judgment
\Gamma\vdash t:P
.
For each pair of dual rules, we only study the positive one
(
e.g.
we study
I
{}_{\land}^{+}
but not the dual rule
I
{}_{\lor}^{-}
):

1.

Ax
:
Let
\Gamma,x:P\vdash x:P

and
\sigma\vDash_{\rho}\Gamma,x:P
.
Then
|x|^{\sigma}=\sigma(x)\in[\![P]\!]_{\rho}

by the fact that
\sigma
is adequate.

2.

Abs
:
Let

\Gamma\vdash t\mathrel{{\RHD\!\!\!\LHD_{P}}}s:P

be derived from
\Gamma\vdash t:A{}^{+}

and
\Gamma\vdash s:A{}^{-}
,
and let
\sigma\vDash_{\rho}\Gamma
.
By IH we have that

|t|^{\sigma}\in[\![A{}^{+}]\!]_{\rho}

and

|s|^{\sigma}\in[\![A{}^{-}]\!]_{\rho}
.
Recall from Lem.
30

that
([\![A{}^{+}]\!]_{\rho},[\![A{}^{-}]\!]_{\rho})\in\bm{\,\perp\!\!\!\perp}
.
By Lem.
32
,

(|t|^{\sigma}\mathrel{{\RHD\!\!\!\LHD}}|s|^{\sigma})\in[\![P]\!]_{\rho}
.

3.

I
{}_{\circ}^{+}
:
Let
\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{\textup{\Circle}}{}^{+}_{(x:A{}^{\ominus})}.\,t:A{}^{\oplus}

be derived from
\Gamma,x:A{}^{\ominus}\vdash t:A{}^{+}
,
and let
\sigma\vDash_{\rho}\Gamma
.
By IH, for every substitution
\sigma^{\prime}

such that
\sigma^{\prime}\vDash_{\rho}\Gamma,x:A{}^{\ominus}

we have that
|t|^{\sigma^{\prime}}\in[\![A{}^{+}]\!]_{\rho}
.
In particular, for every
a\in[\![A{}^{\ominus}]\!]_{\rho}

we have that
|t|^{\sigma[x:=a]}\in[\![A{}^{+}]\!]_{\rho}
.
Moreover, note that

|t|^{\sigma[x:=a]}=|t|^{\sigma[x:=x]}\{x:=a\}
.
Hence by Lem.
37

we have that

|\mathsf{\textup{\Circle}}{}^{+}_{(x:A{}^{\ominus})}.\,t|^{\sigma}=\lambda_{x}.\,|t|^{\sigma[x:=x]}\in[\![A{}^{\ominus}]\!]_{\rho}\bm{\rightarrow}[\![A{}^{+}]\!]_{\rho}
.
To conclude, recall from Lem.
29

that
[\![A{}^{\ominus}]\!]_{\rho}\bm{\rightarrow}[\![A{}^{+}]\!]_{\rho}=[\![A{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho}
,
so

|\mathsf{\textup{\Circle}}{}^{+}_{(x:A{}^{\ominus})}.\,t|^{\sigma}\in[\![A{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho}
.

4.

E
{}_{\circ}^{+}
:
Let
\Gamma\vdash t\bullet\!\!{}^{+}\,s:A{}^{+}

be derived from
\Gamma\vdash t:A{}^{\oplus}

and
\Gamma\vdash s:A{}^{\ominus}
,
and let
\sigma\vDash_{\rho}\Gamma
.
By IH,
|t|^{\sigma}\in[\![A{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho}

and
|s|^{\sigma}\in[\![A{}^{\ominus}]\!]_{\rho}
.
Recall from Lem.
29

that
[\![A{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho}=[\![A{}^{\ominus}]\!]_{\rho}\bm{\rightarrow}[\![A{}^{+}]\!]_{\rho}
,
so

|t\bullet\!\!{}^{+}\,s|^{\sigma}=|t|^{\sigma}@|s|^{\sigma}\in[\![A{}^{+}]\!]_{\rho}
.

5.

I
{}_{\land}^{+}
:
Let
\Gamma\vdash\langle t,s\rangle{}^{+}:(A\land B){}^{+}

be derived from
\Gamma\vdash t:A{}^{\oplus}

and
\Gamma\vdash s:B{}^{\oplus}
,
and let
\sigma\vDash_{\rho}\Gamma
.
By IH,
|t|^{\sigma}\in[\![A{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho}

and
|s|^{\sigma}\in[\![B{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho}
.
By Lem.
33
,

\langle|t|^{\sigma},|s|^{\sigma}\rangle\in[\![A{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho}\bm{\times}[\![B{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho}=[\![(A\land B){}^{+}]\!]_{\rho}
.

6.

E
{}^{+}_{\land{i}}
:
Let
\Gamma\vdash\pi_{i}^{+}(t):A_{i}{}^{\oplus}

be derived from

\Gamma\vdash t:(A_{1}\land A_{2}){}^{+}

for some
i\in\{1,2\}
,
and let
\sigma\vDash_{\rho}\Gamma
.
By IH,

|t|^{\sigma}\in[\![(A_{1}\land A_{2}){}^{+}]\!]_{\rho}=[\![A_{1}{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho}\bm{\times}[\![A_{2}{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho}
.
By Lem.
34
,

\pi_{i}(|t|^{\sigma})\in[\![A_{i}{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho}
.

7.

I
{}^{+}_{\lor{i}}
:
Let
\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{in}_{i}\!\!{}^{+}(t):(A_{1}\lor A_{2}){}^{+}

be derived from
\Gamma\vdash t:A_{i}{}^{\oplus}

for some
i\in\{1,2\}
,
and let
\sigma\vDash_{\rho}\Gamma
.
By IH,
|t|^{\sigma}\in[\![A_{i}{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho}
.
By Lem.
35
,

\mathsf{in}_{i}(|t|^{\sigma})\in[\![A_{1}{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho}\bm{+}[\![A_{2}{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho}=[\![(A_{1}\lor A_{2}){}^{+}]\!]_{\rho}
.

8.

E
{}_{\lor}^{+}
:
Let

\Gamma\vdash\delta{}^{+}t\,[_{x:A{}^{\oplus}}.s][_{y:B{}^{\oplus}}.u]:P

be derived from

\Gamma\vdash t:(A\lor B){}^{+}

and

\Gamma,x:A{}^{\oplus}\vdash s:P

and

\Gamma,y:B{}^{\oplus}\vdash u:P
,
and let
\sigma\vDash_{\rho}\Gamma
.
By IH on the first premise,

|t|^{\sigma}\in[\![(A\lor B){}^{+}]\!]_{\rho}=[\![A{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho}\bm{+}[\![B{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho}
.
By IH on the second premise,
we have that

|s|^{\sigma^{\prime}}\in[\![P]\!]_{\rho}

for every substitution
\sigma^{\prime}

such that
\sigma^{\prime}\vDash_{\rho}\Gamma,x:A{}^{\oplus}
.
In particular, for every
a\in[\![A{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho}

we have that

|s|^{\sigma[x:=a]}\in[\![P]\!]_{\rho}
.
Moreover, note that

|s|^{\sigma[x:=a]}=|s|^{\sigma[x:=x]}\{x:=a\}
.
Similarly, by IH on the third premise,
for every
b\in[\![B{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho}
,
we have that

|u|^{\sigma[y:=y]}\{y:=b\}\in[\![P]\!]_{\rho}
.
Hence by Lem.
36
,
we have that

|\delta{}^{+}t\,[_{x:A{}^{\oplus}}.s][_{y:B{}^{\oplus}}.u]|^{\sigma}=\delta|t|^{\sigma}\,[_{x}.|s|^{\sigma[x:=x]}][_{y}.|u|^{\sigma[y:=y]}]\in[\![P]\!]_{\rho}
,
as required.

9.

I
{}_{\rightarrow}^{+}
:
Let

\Gamma\vdash\lambda{}^{+}_{(x:A{}^{\oplus})}.{t}:(A\rightarrow B){}^{+}

be derived from

\Gamma,x:A{}^{\oplus}\vdash t:B{}^{\oplus}
,
and let
\sigma\vDash_{\rho}\Gamma
.
By IH, we have that
|t|^{\sigma^{\prime}}\in[\![B{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho}

for every substitution
\sigma^{\prime}
such that

\sigma^{\prime}\vDash_{\rho}\Gamma,x:A{}^{\oplus}
.
In particular, for every
a\in[\![A{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho}

we have that

|t|^{\sigma[x:=a]}\in[\![B{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho}
.
Moreover, note that

|t|^{\sigma[x:=a]}=|t|^{\sigma[x:=x]}\{x:=a\}
.
Hence by Lem.
37

we have that

|\lambda{}^{+}_{(x:A{}^{\oplus})}.{t}|^{\sigma}=\lambda_{x}.\,|t|^{\sigma[x:=x]}\in([\![A{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho}\bm{\rightarrow}[\![B{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho})=[\![(A\rightarrow B){}^{+}]\!]_{\rho}
.

10.

E
{}_{\rightarrow}^{+}
:
Let

\Gamma\vdash t@{}^{+}s:B{}^{\oplus}

be derived from
\Gamma\vdash t:(A\rightarrow B){}^{+}

and
\Gamma\vdash s:A{}^{\oplus}
,
and let
\sigma\vDash_{\rho}\Gamma
.
By IH on the first premise,

|t|^{\sigma}\in[\![(A\rightarrow B){}^{+}]\!]_{\rho}=[\![A{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho}\bm{\rightarrow}[\![B{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho}
.
By IH on the second premise,

|s|^{\sigma}\in[\![A{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho}
.
By definition of the arrow operator,

|t|^{\sigma}@|s|^{\sigma}\in[\![B{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho}
,
as required.

11.

I
{}_{\ltimes}^{+}
:
Let
\Gamma\vdash(t\,\bm{;}\!{}^{+}s):(A\ltimes B){}^{+}

be derived from
\Gamma\vdash t:A{}^{\ominus}

and
\Gamma\vdash s:B{}^{\oplus}
.
By IH,
|t|^{\sigma}\in[\![A{}^{\ominus}]\!]_{\rho}

and
|s|^{\sigma}\in[\![B{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho}
.
By Lem.
33
,

(|t|^{\sigma}\,\bm{;}|s|^{\sigma})\in[\![A{}^{\ominus}]\!]_{\rho}\bm{\times}[\![B{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho}=[\![(A\ltimes B){}^{+}]\!]_{\rho}
.

12.

E
{}_{\ltimes}^{+}
:
Let
\Gamma\vdash\varrho^{+}{t}[_{x;y}.{s}]:P

be derived from

\Gamma\vdash t:(A\ltimes B){}^{+}

and

\Gamma,x:A{}^{\ominus},y:B{}^{\oplus}\vdash s:P
,
and let
\sigma\vDash_{\rho}\Gamma
.
By IH on the first premise,

|t|^{\sigma}\in[\![(A\ltimes B){}^{+}]\!]_{\rho}=[\![A{}^{\ominus}]\!]_{\rho}\ltimes[\![B{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho}
.
By IH on the second premise,
we have that

|s|^{\sigma^{\prime}}\in[\![P]\!]_{\rho}

for every substitution
\sigma^{\prime}

such that
\sigma^{\prime}\vDash_{\rho}\Gamma,x:A{}^{\ominus},y:B{}^{\oplus}
.
In particular,
for every
a_{1}\in[\![A{}^{\ominus}]\!]_{\rho}

and every
a_{2}\in[\![B{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho}

we have that

|s|^{\sigma[x:=a_{1}][y:=a_{2}]}\in[\![P]\!]_{\rho}

Moreover, note that

|s|^{\sigma[x:=a_{1}][y:=a_{2}]}=|s|^{\sigma[x:=x][y:=y]}\{x:=a_{1}\}\{y:=a_{2}\}
.
Hence by Lem.
39
,
we have that

|\varrho^{+}{t}[_{x;y}.{s}]|^{\sigma}=\varrho{|t|^{\sigma}}[_{x;y}.{|s|^{\sigma[x:=x][y:=y]}}]\in[\![P]\!]_{\rho}
,
as required.

13.

I
{}_{\lnot}^{+}
:
Let
\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{N}{}^{+}t:(\neg A){}^{+}

be derived from
\Gamma\vdash t:A{}^{\ominus}
,
and let
\sigma\vDash_{\rho}\Gamma
.
By IH,
|t|^{\sigma}\in[\![A{}^{\ominus}]\!]_{\rho}
.
By Lem.
38
,

\mathsf{N}|t|^{\sigma}\in\bm{\mathord{\sim}}[\![A{}^{\ominus}]\!]_{\rho}=[\![(\neg A){}^{+}]\!]_{\rho}
.

14.

E
{}_{\lnot}^{+}
:
Let
\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{M}{}^{+}t:A{}^{\ominus}

be derived from
\Gamma\vdash t:(\neg A){}^{+}
,
and let
\sigma\vDash_{\rho}\Gamma
.
By IH,

|t|^{\sigma}\in[\![(\neg A){}^{+}]\!]_{\rho}=\bm{\mathord{\sim}}[\![A{}^{\ominus}]\!]_{\rho}
.
By Lem.
40
,

\mathsf{M}|t|^{\sigma}\in[\![A{}^{\ominus}]\!]_{\rho}
.

15.

I
{}_{\forall}^{+}
:
Let

\Gamma\vdash\lambda{}^{+}_{\alpha}.\,t:(\forall\alpha.\,A){}^{+}

be derived from

\Gamma\vdash t:A{}^{\oplus}
,
where we assume that
\alpha\notin\mathsf{ftv}(\Gamma)
,
and let
\sigma\vDash_{\rho}\Gamma
.
By IH,
for every environment
\rho^{\prime}

and every substitution
\sigma^{\prime}

such that
\sigma^{\prime}\vDash_{\rho^{\prime}}\Gamma
,
we have that
|t|^{\sigma^{\prime}}\in[\![A{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho^{\prime}}
.
In particular, consider two arbitrary orthogonal reducibility candidates

(\xi{}^{+},\xi{}^{-})\in\bm{\,\perp\!\!\!\perp}
,
consider the environment
\rho^{\prime}=\rho[\alpha:=\xi{}^{+},\xi{}^{-}]
,
and observe that
\sigma\vDash_{\rho^{\prime}}\Gamma
,
because if
(x:P)\in\Gamma

by irrelevance Lem.
27

\sigma(x)\in[\![A{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho}=[\![A{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho^{\prime}}

since
\alpha\notin\mathsf{ftv}(P)
.
Then

|t|^{\sigma}\in[\![A{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho[\alpha:=\xi{}^{+},\xi{}^{-}]}
,
where
(\xi{}^{+},\xi{}^{-})\in\bm{\,\perp\!\!\!\perp}
are arbitrary.
To conclude note that, by Lem.
41
,

\lambda_{\Diamond}.\,|t|^{\sigma}\in\bm{\Pi}_{(\xi{}^{+},\xi{}^{-})\in\bm{\,\perp\!\!\!\perp}}\,{[\![A{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho[\alpha:=\xi{}^{+},\xi{}^{-}]}}=[\![(\forall\alpha.\,A){}^{+}]\!]_{\rho}
.

16.

E
{}_{\forall}^{+}
:
Let

\Gamma\vdash t@{}^{+}A:B{}^{\oplus}\{\alpha:=A\}

be derived from

\Gamma\vdash t:(\forall\alpha.\,B){}^{+}
,
and let
\sigma\vDash_{\rho}\Gamma
.
By IH,

|t|^{\sigma}\in[\![(\forall\alpha.\,B){}^{+}]\!]_{\rho}=\bm{\Pi}_{(\xi{}^{+},\xi{}^{-})\in\bm{\,\perp\!\!\!\perp}}\,{[\![B{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho[\alpha:=\xi{}^{+},\xi{}^{-}]}}
.
By definition of the indexed product, this means that

|t|^{\sigma}@\Diamond\in[\![B{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho[\alpha:=\xi_{0}{}^{+},\xi_{0}{}^{-}]}

for an arbitrary choice of

(\xi_{0}{}^{+},\xi_{0}{}^{-})\in\bm{\,\perp\!\!\!\perp}
.
Recall from Lem.
30

that
([\![A{}^{+}]\!]_{\rho},[\![A{}^{-}]\!]_{\rho})\in\bm{\,\perp\!\!\!\perp}

so, in particular,
taking
\xi_{0}{}^{+}:=[\![A{}^{+}]\!]_{\rho}

and
\xi_{0}{}^{-}:=[\![A{}^{-}]\!]_{\rho}
,
we have that

|t|^{\sigma}@\Diamond\in[\![B{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho[\alpha:=[\![A{}^{+}]\!]_{\rho},[\![A{}^{-}]\!]_{\rho}]}
.
To conclude, observe that by Lem.
28

[\![B{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho[\alpha:=[\![A{}^{+}]\!]_{\rho},[\![A{}^{-}]\!]_{\rho}]}=[\![B{}^{\oplus}\{\alpha:=A\}]\!]_{\rho}
.
Hence

|t|^{\sigma}@\Diamond\in[\![B{}^{\oplus}\{\alpha:=A\}]\!]_{\rho}
,
as required.

17.

I
{}_{\exists}^{+}
:
Let
\Gamma\vdash\langle A,t\rangle{}^{+}:(\exists\alpha.\,B){}^{+}

be derived from
\Gamma\vdash t:B{}^{\oplus}\{\alpha:=A\}
,
and let
\sigma\vDash_{\rho}\Gamma
.
By IH,
|t|^{\sigma}\in[\![B{}^{\oplus}\{\alpha:=A\}]\!]_{\rho}
.
By Lem.
28
, note that:

[\![B{}^{\oplus}\{\alpha:=A\}]\!]_{\rho}=[\![B{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho[\alpha:=[\![A{}^{+}]\!]_{\rho},[\![A{}^{-}]\!]_{\rho}]}

so

|t|^{\sigma}\in[\![B{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho[\alpha:=[\![A{}^{+}]\!]_{\rho},[\![A{}^{-}]\!]_{\rho}]}
.
Recall from Lem.
30

that
([\![A{}^{+}]\!]_{\rho},[\![A{}^{-}]\!]_{\rho})\in\bm{\,\perp\!\!\!\perp}
.
Hence, by Lem.
42
,

\langle\Diamond,|t|^{\sigma}\rangle\in\bm{\Sigma}_{(\xi{}^{+},\xi{}^{-})\in\bm{\,\perp\!\!\!\perp}}\,{[\![B{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho[\alpha:=\xi{}^{+},\xi{}^{-}]}}=[\![(\exists\alpha.\,B){}^{+}]\!]_{\rho}
.

18.

E
{}_{\exists}^{+}
:
Let
\Gamma\vdash\nabla{}^{+}\,t[_{(\alpha,x)}.s]:P

be derived from
\Gamma\vdash t:(\exists\alpha.\,A){}^{+}

and
\Gamma,x:A{}^{\oplus}\vdash s:P
,
where we assume that
\alpha\notin\mathsf{ftv}(\Gamma,P)
.
Moreover, let
\sigma\vDash_{\rho}\Gamma
.
By IH on the first premise, we have that:

|t|^{\sigma}\in[\![(\exists\alpha.\,A){}^{+}]\!]_{\rho}=\bm{\Sigma}_{(\xi{}^{+},\xi{}^{-})\in\bm{\,\perp\!\!\!\perp}}\,{[\![A{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho[\alpha:=\xi{}^{+},\xi{}^{-}]}}

By IH on the second premise,
for every environment
\rho^{\prime}
and every substitution
\sigma^{\prime}

such that
\sigma^{\prime}\vDash_{\rho^{\prime}}\Gamma,x:A{}^{\oplus}
,
we have that

|s|^{\sigma^{\prime}}\in[\![P]\!]_{\rho^{\prime}}
.
In particular, consider two arbitrary orthogonal reducibility
candidates
(\xi{}^{+},\xi{}^{-})\in\bm{\,\perp\!\!\!\perp}
,
and consider the environment
\rho^{\prime}=\rho[\alpha:=\xi{}^{+},\xi{}^{-}]
.
By hypothesis
\alpha\notin\mathsf{ftv}(P)
,
so by irrelevance (Lem.
27
)
we have that
[\![P]\!]_{\rho}=[\![P]\!]_{\rho^{\prime}}
.
Moreover, consider an arbitrary term
a\in[\![A{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho^{\prime}}
,
and consider the substitution
\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma[x:=a]
.

We argue that
\sigma^{\prime}\vDash_{\rho^{\prime}}\Gamma,x:A{}^{\oplus}
holds.
To see this, note, on one hand,
that
\sigma^{\prime}(x)=a\in[\![A{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho^{\prime}}
.
On the other hand, given an association
(y:Q)\in\Gamma

for a variable other than
x
,
note that by hypothesis

\sigma^{\prime}(y)=\sigma(y)\in[\![Q]\!]_{\rho}
.
Moreover, by hypothesis
\alpha\notin\mathsf{ftv}(Q)
,
so by irrelevance (Lem.
27
)
we have that
[\![Q]\!]_{\rho}=[\![Q]\!]_{\rho^{\prime}}
.

In summary, the IH on the second premise tells us that
for all
(\xi{}^{+},\xi{}^{-})\in\bm{\,\perp\!\!\!\perp}
,
and for all
a\in[\![A{}^{\oplus}]\!]_{\rho[\alpha:=\xi{}^{+},\xi{}^{-}]}
,
we have

|s|^{\sigma[x:=a]}\in[\![P]\!]_{\rho}
.
Note that:

|s|^{\sigma[x:=a]}=|s|^{\sigma[x:=x]}\{x:=a\}

Finally, by Lem.
43
,
we conclude that

|\nabla{}^{+}\,t[_{(\alpha,x)}.s]|^{\sigma}=\nabla\,|t|^{\sigma}[_{(\Diamond,x)}.|s|^{\sigma[x:=x]}]\in[\![P]\!]_{\rho}
,
as required.

∎

Corollary 46

(Strong normalization)
.

Let
\Gamma\vdash t:P
.
Then
t
is strongly normalizing.

Proof.

Consider the environment
\rho
that maps all type variables
to the bottom reducibility candidate,
that is
\rho(\alpha{}^{+})=\rho(\alpha{}^{-})=\bm{\bot}

for every type variable
\alpha
.
Note that this is indeed an environment because

(\bm{\bot},\bm{\bot})\in\bm{\,\perp\!\!\!\perp}
.
Moreover, let
\sigma
be the identity substitution,
that is,
\sigma(x)=x
for all variables
x
.
Remark that
\sigma\vDash_{\rho}\Gamma

because a variable
x
is in any reducibility candidate,
so if
(x:Q)\in\Gamma
, then indeed
x\in[\![Q]\!]_{\rho}
.
Then, by adequacy Thm.
5
, we have that

|t|\in[\![P]\!]_{\rho}
,
so in particular
|t|
is strongly normalizing with respect to
\to_{\mathbf{U}}
.
Finally, recall from
that this entails that
t
is strongly normalizing with respect to
\xrightarrow{}
.
∎

Appendix F
Admissible Principles in
\lambda^{\textup{PRJ}}

Definition 47
.

X
-intuitionistic term
If
X
is an arbitrary set of variables,
a term
t
is
X
-intuitionistic
if it is intuitionistic and, furthermore,
it has no useful free occurrences of variables in
X
.
Remark that a term is intuitionistic if and only if it is

\varnothing
-intuitionistic.

If
X
is an arbitrary set of variables,
we generalize the judgment
\Gamma\vdash_{\textup{PRJ}}t:A

by writing
\Gamma\vdash^{X}_{\textup{PRJ}}t:A
,
if the judgment is derivable in
PRK
and
t
is
X
-intuitionistic.

Lemma 48

(Admissible Principles in
\lambda^{\textup{PRJ}}
)
.

1.

Counterfactual weakening.

If
\Gamma\vdash^{X}_{\textup{PRJ}}t:P

and
X\supseteq X^{\prime}

then
\Gamma\vdash^{X^{\prime}}_{\textup{PRJ}}t:P
.

2.

Weakening
(
W
):
If
\Gamma\vdash^{X}_{\textup{PRJ}}t:P

and
x\notin\mathsf{fv}(t)

then
\Gamma,x:Q\vdash^{X}_{\textup{PRJ}}t:P
.

3.

Intuitionistic cut
(
ICut
):
If
\Gamma,x:P\vdash^{X}_{\textup{PRJ}}t:Q

and
\Gamma\vdash^{X}_{\textup{PRJ}}s:P

then
\Gamma\vdash^{X}_{\textup{PRJ}}t\{x:=s\}:Q
.

4.

Counterfactual cut
(
CCut
):
If
\Gamma,x:P\vdash^{X\cup\{x\}}_{\textup{PRJ}}t:Q

and
\Gamma\vdash_{\textup{PRK}}s:P
,
then
\Gamma\vdash^{X}_{\textup{PRJ}}t\{x:=s\}:Q
.

5.

Generalized absurdity
(
\textup{{Abs}}^{\prime}
):
If
\Gamma\vdash^{X}_{\textup{PRJ}}t:P

and
\Gamma\vdash^{X}_{\textup{PRJ}}s:P{}^{\sim}
,
where
P
is not necessarily strong,
there is a term
t\mathrel{\bowtie_{Q}}s

such that
\Gamma\vdash^{X}_{\textup{PRJ}}t\mathrel{\bowtie_{Q}}s:Q
.

6.

Intuitionistic contraposition
(
IContra
):
Let
\Gamma,x:A{}^{\oplus}\vdash^{X}_{\textup{PRJ}}t:Q

and suppose that
y\notin X\cup\mathsf{fv}(t)
.
Then there is a term
{\mathtt{ic}_{x}^{y}(t)}

such that

\Gamma,y:Q{}^{\sim}\vdash^{X}_{\textup{PRJ}}{\mathtt{ic}_{x}^{y}(t)}:A{}^{\ominus}
.

7.

Counterfactual contraposition
(
CContra
):

\Gamma,x:A{}^{\ominus}\vdash^{X\cup\{x\}}_{\textup{PRJ}}t:Q
,
there is a term
{\mathtt{cc}_{x}^{y}(t)}

such that

\Gamma,y:Q{}^{\sim}\vdash^{X}_{\textup{PRJ}}{\mathtt{cc}_{x}^{y}(t)}:A{}^{\oplus}
.

8.

Weak negation introduction
:
If
\Gamma\vdash_{\textup{PRJ}}t:A{}^{\ominus}
,
there is a term
\mathsf{N}{}^{\oplus}t

such that
\Gamma\vdash_{\textup{PRJ}}\mathsf{N}{}^{\oplus}t:(\neg A){}^{\oplus}
.

9.

Weak negation elimination
:
If
\Gamma\vdash_{\textup{PRJ}}t:(\neg A){}^{\oplus}
,
there is a term
\mathsf{M}{}^{\oplus}t

such that
\Gamma\vdash_{\textup{PRJ}}\mathsf{M}{}^{\oplus}t:A{}^{\ominus}
.

Proof.

Counterfactual weakening
,
weakening
,
cut
,
and
counterfactual cut
are straightforward by induction
on the derivation of the given judgment.

For
generalized absurdity
, it suffices to take:

t\mathrel{\bowtie_{Q}}s\,\mathrel{\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}}\,\begin{cases}t\mathrel{{\RHD\!\!\!\LHD_{Q}}}s&\text{if $P=A{}^{+}$}\\
s\mathrel{{\RHD\!\!\!\LHD_{Q}}}t&\text{if $P=A{}^{-}$}\\
(t\bullet\!\!{}^{+}\,s)\mathrel{{\RHD\!\!\!\LHD_{Q}}}(s\bullet\!\!{}^{-}\,t)&\text{if $P=A{}^{\oplus}$}\\
(s\bullet\!\!{}^{+}\,t)\mathrel{{\RHD\!\!\!\LHD_{Q}}}(t\bullet\!\!{}^{-}\,s)&\text{if $P=A{}^{\ominus}$}\\
\end{cases}

For
intuitionistic contraposition
, take the term below.
Observe that no condition must be imposed on the free occurrences
of
x
in
t
, because
x
is a
positive
counterfactual:

{\mathtt{ic}_{x}^{y}(t)}\,\mathrel{\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}}\,\mathsf{\textup{\Circle}}{}^{-}_{(x:A{}^{\oplus})}.\,(t\mathrel{\bowtie_{A{}^{-}}}y)

For
counterfactual contraposition
, take the term below.
Observe that
x
is a
negative
counterfactual, and this is
why we require in the hypothesis that
x
has no useful free occurrences
in
t
:

{\mathtt{cc}_{x}^{y}(t)}\,\mathrel{\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}}\,\mathsf{\textup{\Circle}}{}^{+}_{(x:A{}^{\ominus})}.\,(t\mathrel{\bowtie_{A{}^{+}}}y)

For
weak negation introduction
,
let
\Gamma\vdash_{\textup{PRJ}}t:A{}^{\ominus}
and take the term below.
Observe that there are no free occurrences of the negative counterfactual
in
\mathsf{N}{}^{+}t
:

\mathsf{N}{}^{\oplus}t\,\mathrel{\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}}\,\mathsf{\textup{\Circle}}{}^{+}_{(\underline{\,\,\,}:(\neg A){}^{\ominus})}.\,\mathsf{N}{}^{+}t

For
weak negation elimination
,
let
\Gamma\vdash_{\textup{PRJ}}t:(\neg A){}^{\oplus}
and take
the term below. Observe that all the occurrences
of the negative counterfactual
x
are useless:

\mathsf{M}{}^{\oplus}t\,\mathrel{\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}}\,\mathsf{\textup{\Circle}}{}^{-}_{(x:A{}^{\oplus})}.\,\mathsf{M}{}^{+}(t\bullet\!\!{}^{+}\,\mathsf{\textup{\Circle}}{}^{-}_{\underline{\,\,\,}:(\neg A){}^{\oplus}}.\,\mathsf{N}{}^{-}x)\bullet\!\!^{\pm}\,x

∎

Appendix G
An Alternative Presentation of
\lambda^{\textup{PRJ}}
: the
\lambda^{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}
Type System

The
\lambda^{\textup{PRJ}}
type system
is formulated by imposing a global restriction on terms,
forbidding that subterms of specific forms occur in specific positions.
Alternatively, valid judgments in
PRJ
may be defined inductively
with inference rules, as shown in this section.

Definition 49

(The
\lambda^{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}
type system)
.

The type system
\lambda^{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}

is defined with typing judgments of the form “
\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:P
”
for each set of variables
X

by means of inductive rules below.
We write
\Gamma\vdash^{X}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t:A

if
\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:A
is derivable in
PRJ
\star
.

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 20.10817pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty x\notin X$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.54375pt,depth=-2.08122pt,width=65.69333pt\hbox{\kern 2.83339pt$\textsc{\footnotesize{{{Ax}}$\mathtt{J}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma,x:P\vdash^{X}x:P$}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:A{}^{+}\hskip 14.22636pt\Gamma\vdash^{X}s:A{}^{-}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.54375pt,depth=-2.08122pt,width=103.27774pt\hbox{\kern 2.83339pt$\textsc{\footnotesize{{{Abs}}$\mathtt{J}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 21.55388pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash^{X}t\mathrel{{\RHD\!\!\!\LHD_{P}}}s:P$}}}}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma,x:A{}^{\ominus}\vdash^{X\cup\{x\}}t:A{}^{+}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.54375pt,depth=-2.08122pt,width=93.31712pt\hbox{\kern 2.83339pt$\textsc{\footnotesize{{{I${}_{\circ}^{+}$}}$\mathtt{J}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 5.5732pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash^{X}\mathsf{\textup{\Circle}}{}^{+}_{(x:A{}^{\ominus})}.\,t:A{}^{\oplus}$}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 5.88354pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma,x:A{}^{\oplus}\vdash^{X}t:A{}^{-}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.54375pt,depth=-2.08122pt,width=82.17073pt\hbox{\kern 2.83339pt$\textsc{\footnotesize{{{I${}_{\circ}^{-}$}}$\mathtt{J}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash^{X}\mathsf{\textup{\Circle}}{}^{+}_{(x:A{}^{\oplus})}.\,t:A{}^{\ominus}$}}}}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:A{}^{\oplus}\hskip 14.22636pt\Gamma\vdash_{\textup{PRK}}s:A{}^{\ominus}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.54375pt,depth=-2.08122pt,width=116.67093pt\hbox{\kern 2.83339pt$\textsc{\footnotesize{{{E${}_{\circ}^{+}$}}$\mathtt{J}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 27.55759pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash^{X}t\bullet\!\!{}^{+}\,s:A{}^{+}$}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:A{}^{\ominus}\hskip 14.22636pt\Gamma\vdash^{X}s:A{}^{\oplus}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.54375pt,depth=-2.08122pt,width=106.89442pt\hbox{\kern 2.83339pt$\textsc{\footnotesize{{{E${}_{\circ}^{-}$}}$\mathtt{J}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 25.93597pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash^{X}t\bullet\!\!{}^{-}\,s:A{}^{-}$}}}}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:A{}^{\oplus}\hskip 14.22636pt\Gamma\vdash^{X}s:B{}^{\oplus}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.54375pt,depth=-2.08122pt,width=107.40587pt\hbox{\kern 2.83339pt$\textsc{\footnotesize{{{I${}_{\land}^{+}$}}$\mathtt{J}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 8.39578pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash^{X}\langle t,s\rangle{}^{+}:(A\land B){}^{+}$}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:A{}^{\ominus}\hskip 14.22636pt\Gamma\vdash^{X}s:B{}^{\ominus}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.54375pt,depth=-2.08122pt,width=107.40587pt\hbox{\kern 2.83339pt$\textsc{\footnotesize{{{I${}_{\lor}^{-}$}}$\mathtt{J}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 11.66243pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash^{X}\langle t,s\rangle{}^{-}:(A\lor B){}^{-}$}}}}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:(A_{1}\land A_{2}){}^{+}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.54375pt,depth=-2.08122pt,width=76.72714pt\hbox{\kern 2.83339pt$\textsc{\footnotesize{{{E${}^{+}_{\land{i}}$}}$\mathtt{J}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 4.34508pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash^{X}\pi_{i}^{+}(t):A_{i}{}^{\oplus}$}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:(A_{1}\lor A_{2}){}^{-}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.54375pt,depth=-2.08122pt,width=73.4605pt\hbox{\kern 2.83339pt$\textsc{\footnotesize{{{E${}^{-}_{\lor{i}}$}}$\mathtt{J}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 4.2539pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash^{X}\pi_{i}^{-}(t):A_{i}{}^{\ominus}$}}}}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 24.22476pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:A_{i}{}^{\oplus}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.54375pt,depth=-2.08122pt,width=97.60646pt\hbox{\kern 2.83339pt$\textsc{\footnotesize{{{I${}^{+}_{\lor{i}}$}}$\mathtt{J}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash^{X}\mathsf{in}_{i}\!\!{}^{+}(t):(A_{1}\lor A_{2}){}^{+}$}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 20.95811pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:A_{i}{}^{\ominus}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.54375pt,depth=-2.08122pt,width=91.07317pt\hbox{\kern 2.83339pt$\textsc{\footnotesize{{{I${}^{-}_{\land{i}}$}}$\mathtt{J}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash^{X}\mathsf{in}_{i}\!\!{}^{-}(t):(A_{1}\land A_{2}){}^{-}$}}}}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:(A\lor B){}^{+}\hskip 14.22636pt\Gamma,x:A{}^{\oplus}\vdash^{X}s:P\hskip 14.22636pt\Gamma,y:B{}^{\oplus}\vdash^{X}u:P$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.54375pt,depth=-2.08122pt,width=237.21161pt\hbox{\kern 2.83339pt$\textsc{\footnotesize{{{E${}_{\lor}^{+}$}}$\mathtt{J}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 61.77142pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash^{X}\delta{}^{+}t\,[_{x:A{}^{\oplus}}.s][_{y:B{}^{\oplus}}.u]:P$}}}}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 12.06055pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma,x:A{}^{\oplus}\vdash^{X}t:B{}^{\oplus}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.54375pt,depth=-2.08122pt,width=98.47784pt\hbox{\kern 2.83339pt$\textsc{\footnotesize{{{I${}_{\rightarrow}^{+}$}}$\mathtt{J}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash^{X}\lambda{}^{+}_{(x:A{}^{\oplus})}.{t}:(A\rightarrow B){}^{+}$}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 11.19804pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma,x:A{}^{\ominus}\vdash^{X}t:B{}^{\ominus}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.54375pt,depth=-2.08122pt,width=96.75284pt\hbox{\kern 2.83339pt$\textsc{\footnotesize{{{I${}_{\ltimes}^{-}$}}$\mathtt{J}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash^{X}\lambda{}^{-}_{(x:A{}^{\ominus})}.{t}:(A\ltimes B){}^{-}$}}}}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:(A\rightarrow B){}^{+}\hskip 14.22636pt\Gamma\vdash^{X}s:A{}^{\oplus}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.54375pt,depth=-2.08122pt,width=131.12512pt\hbox{\kern 2.83339pt$\textsc{\footnotesize{{{E${}_{\rightarrow}^{+}$}}$\mathtt{J}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 33.43451pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash^{X}t@{}^{+}s:B{}^{\oplus}$}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:(A\ltimes B){}^{-}\hskip 14.22636pt\Gamma\vdash^{X}s:A{}^{\ominus}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.54375pt,depth=-2.08122pt,width=129.40013pt\hbox{\kern 2.83339pt$\textsc{\footnotesize{{{E${}_{\ltimes}^{-}$}}$\mathtt{J}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 34.20534pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash^{X}t@{}^{-}s:B{}^{\ominus}$}}}}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:A{}^{\ominus}\hskip 14.22636pt\Gamma\vdash^{X}s:B{}^{\oplus}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.54375pt,depth=-2.08122pt,width=107.40587pt\hbox{\kern 2.83339pt$\textsc{\footnotesize{{{I${}_{\ltimes}^{+}$}}$\mathtt{J}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 7.46527pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash^{X}(t\,\bm{;}\!{}^{+}s):(A\ltimes B){}^{+}$}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:A{}^{\oplus}\hskip 14.22636pt\Gamma\vdash^{X}s:B{}^{\ominus}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.54375pt,depth=-2.08122pt,width=107.40587pt\hbox{\kern 2.83339pt$\textsc{\footnotesize{{{I${}_{\rightarrow}^{-}$}}$\mathtt{J}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 11.50275pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash^{X}(t\,\bm{;}\!{}^{-}s):(A\rightarrow B){}^{-}$}}}}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:(A\ltimes B){}^{+}\hskip 14.22636pt\Gamma,x:A{}^{\ominus},y:B{}^{\oplus}\vdash^{X}s:P$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.54375pt,depth=-2.08122pt,width=185.06224pt\hbox{\kern 2.83339pt$\textsc{\footnotesize{{{E${}_{\ltimes}^{+}$}}$\mathtt{J}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 55.51141pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash^{X}\varrho^{+}{t}[_{x;y}.{s}]:P$}}}}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 13.83044pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:A{}^{\ominus}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.54375pt,depth=-2.08122pt,width=73.51054pt\hbox{\kern 2.83339pt$\textsc{\footnotesize{{{I${}_{\lnot}^{+}$}}$\mathtt{J}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash^{X}\mathsf{N}{}^{+}t:(\neg A){}^{+}$}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 10.5638pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:A{}^{\oplus}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.54375pt,depth=-2.08122pt,width=66.97725pt\hbox{\kern 2.83339pt$\textsc{\footnotesize{{{I${}_{\lnot}^{-}$}}$\mathtt{J}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash^{X}\mathsf{N}{}^{-}t:(\neg A){}^{-}$}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:(\neg A){}^{+}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.54375pt,depth=-2.08122pt,width=60.57732pt\hbox{\kern 2.83339pt$\textsc{\footnotesize{{{E${}_{\lnot}^{+}$}}$\mathtt{J}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.12639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash^{X}\mathsf{M}{}^{+}t:A{}^{\ominus}$}}}}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:A{}^{\oplus}\hskip 14.22636pt\alpha\not\in\mathsf{ftv}(\Gamma)$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.54375pt,depth=-2.08122pt,width=104.89552pt\hbox{\kern 2.83339pt$\textsc{\footnotesize{{{I${}_{\forall}^{+}$}}$\mathtt{J}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 9.74582pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash^{X}\lambda{}^{+}_{\alpha}.\,t:(\forall\alpha.\,A){}^{+}$}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:A{}^{\ominus}\hskip 14.22636pt\alpha\not\in\mathsf{ftv}(\Gamma)$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.54375pt,depth=-2.08122pt,width=104.89552pt\hbox{\kern 2.83339pt$\textsc{\footnotesize{{{I${}_{\exists}^{-}$}}$\mathtt{J}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 11.79073pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash^{X}\lambda{}^{-}_{\alpha}.\,t:(\exists\alpha.\,A){}^{-}$}}}}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 17.29575pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:(\forall\alpha.\,B){}^{+}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.54375pt,depth=-2.08122pt,width=104.65pt\hbox{\kern 2.83339pt$\textsc{\footnotesize{{{E${}_{\forall}^{+}$}}$\mathtt{J}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash^{X}t@{}^{+}A:B{}^{\oplus}\{\alpha:=A\}$}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 17.29575pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:(\exists\alpha.\,B){}^{-}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.54375pt,depth=-2.08122pt,width=101.38335pt\hbox{\kern 2.83339pt$\textsc{\footnotesize{{{E${}_{\exists}^{-}$}}$\mathtt{J}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash^{X}t@{}^{-}A:B{}^{\ominus}\{\alpha:=A\}$}}}}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 4.28883pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:B{}^{\oplus}\{\alpha:=A\}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.54375pt,depth=-2.08122pt,width=92.70561pt\hbox{\kern 2.83339pt$\textsc{\footnotesize{{{I${}_{\exists}^{+}$}}$\mathtt{J}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash^{X}\langle A,t\rangle{}^{+}:(\exists\alpha.\,B){}^{+}$}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 1.02219pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:B{}^{\ominus}\{\alpha:=A\}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.54375pt,depth=-2.08122pt,width=86.17232pt\hbox{\kern 2.83339pt$\textsc{\footnotesize{{{I${}_{\forall}^{-}$}}$\mathtt{J}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash^{X}\langle A,t\rangle{}^{-}:(\forall\alpha.\,B){}^{-}$}}}}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:(\exists\alpha.\,A){}^{+}\hskip 14.22636pt\Gamma,x:A{}^{\oplus}\vdash^{X}s:P\hskip 14.22636pt\alpha\not\in\mathsf{ftv}(\Gamma,P)$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.54375pt,depth=-2.08122pt,width=222.62292pt\hbox{\kern 2.83339pt$\textsc{\footnotesize{{{E${}_{\exists}^{+}$}}$\mathtt{J}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 61.8199pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash^{X}\nabla{}^{+}\,t[_{(\alpha,x:A{}^{\oplus})}.s]:P$}}}}\end{array}

Proposition 50

(Equivalence of
\lambda^{\textup{PRJ}}
and
\lambda^{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}
)
.

The following are equivalent:

1.

\Gamma\vdash^{X}_{\textup{PRJ}}t:A

2.

\Gamma\vdash^{X}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t:A

Proof.

By induction on
t
. We focus on the interesting cases only:

(
\Rightarrow
)

Suppose that
\Gamma\vdash^{X}_{\textup{PRJ}}t:A
,

i.e.
that
\Gamma\vdash_{\textup{PRK}}t:A
and
t
is
X
-intuitionistic.
We proceed by induction on the derivation of the judgment.
The interesting cases are:

1.

Ax
:
Let
\Gamma,x:P\vdash_{\textup{PRK}}x:P
.
Note that
x
is
X
-intuitionistic
and the term
x
contains a useful free occurrence of
x
,
so
x\notin X
.
Hence
\Gamma,x:P\vdash^{X}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}x:P
by
\textup{{Ax}}\mathtt{J}
.

2.

I
{}_{\circ}^{+}
:
Let
\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{\textup{\Circle}}{}^{+}_{(x:A{}^{\ominus})}.\,s:A{}^{\oplus}

be derived from
\Gamma,x:A{}^{\ominus}\vdash s:A{}^{+}
.
Since
t=\mathsf{\textup{\Circle}}{}^{+}_{(x:A{}^{\ominus})}.\,s

is
X
-intuitionistic
we know that
s
is
X
-intuitionistic
and moreover
s
does not have useful free occurrences of
x
.
Hence
s
is
(X\cup\{x\})
-intuitionistic
and by IH we have that

\Gamma,x:A{}^{\ominus}\vdash^{X\cup\{x\}}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}s:A{}^{+}
.
Applying the
\textup{{I${}_{\circ}^{+}$}}\mathtt{J}
rule we conclude that

\Gamma\vdash^{X}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}\mathsf{\textup{\Circle}}{}^{+}_{(x:A{}^{\ominus})}.\,s:A{}^{\oplus}
,
as required.

3.

E
{}_{\circ}^{+}
:
Let
\Gamma\vdash s\bullet\!\!{}^{+}\,u:A{}^{+}

be derived from
\Gamma\vdash s:A{}^{\oplus}

and
\Gamma\vdash u:A{}^{\ominus}
.
Since
t=s\bullet\!\!{}^{+}\,u

is
X
-intuitionistic,
we have that
s
is
X
-intuitionistic.
So, by IH on the first premise, we have

\Gamma\vdash^{X}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}s:A{}^{\oplus}
.
Applying the
\textup{{E${}_{\circ}^{+}$}}\mathtt{J}
rule directly on the second premise,

i.e.
without the need of resorting to the IH for the second premise,
we conclude that
\Gamma\vdash^{X}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}s\bullet\!\!{}^{+}\,u:A{}^{+}
,
as required.

4.

E
{}_{\land}^{-}
:
Then this case is impossible, given that
t

must be of the form

\delta{}^{-}s\,[_{x:A{}^{\ominus}}.u][_{y:B{}^{\ominus}}.r]
,
which is not
X
-intuitionistic,
contradicting the hypothesis.

5.

E
{}_{\rightarrow}^{-}
:
Then this case is impossible, given that
t

must be of the form
\Gamma\vdash\varrho{s}[_{x;y}.{u}]:P
,
which is not
X
-intuitionistic,
contradicting the hypothesis.

6.

E
{}_{\lnot}^{-}
:
Then this case is impossible, given that
t

must be of the form
\mathsf{M}{}^{-}s
,
which is not
X
-intuitionistic,
contradicting the hypothesis.

7.

E
{}_{\forall}^{-}
:
Then this case is impossible, given that
t

must be of the form
\nabla{}^{-}\,s[_{(\alpha,x)}.u]
,
which is not
X
-intuitionistic,
contradicting the hypothesis.

The remaining cases are all straightforward by IH.
For example, for the
I
{}_{\land}^{+}
case,
let
\Gamma\vdash\langle s,u\rangle{}^{+}:(A\land B){}^{+}

be derived from
\Gamma\vdash s:A{}^{\oplus}

and
\Gamma\vdash u:B{}^{\oplus}
.
Since
t=\langle s,u\rangle{}^{+}
is
X
-intuitionistic,

s
and
u
are also
X
-intuitionistic
so, by IH, we have
\Gamma\vdash^{X}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}s:A{}^{\oplus}

and
\Gamma\vdash^{X}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}u:B{}^{\oplus}
.
Applying the
\textup{{I${}_{\land}^{+}$}}\mathtt{J}
rule,
we conclude that

\Gamma\vdash^{X}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}\langle s,u\rangle{}^{+}:(A\land B){}^{+}
,
as required.

(
\Leftarrow
)

By induction on the derivation of
\Gamma\vdash^{X}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t:A
.
The reasoning is similar as for the “only if” direction.

∎

Appendix H
\lambda^{\textup{PRJ}}
Refines Intuitionistic Second-Order Logic

The proof of Thm.
9
,
that
\lambda^{\textup{PRJ}}
refines intuitionistic second-order logic,
is split into two lemmas:

Intuitionistic Conservativity
(Lem.
51
)
proves the implication
1\implies 2
, and

Intuitionistic Embedding
(Lem.
52
)
proves the implication
2\implies 1
.

Recall that
\iota(P)
is defined as follows:

\begin{array}[]{rcl@{\HS}rcl}\iota(A{}^{+})&\,\mathrel{\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}}&A\hfil\hskip 14.22636pt&\iota(A{}^{\oplus})&\,\mathrel{\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}}&A\\
\iota(A{}^{-})&\,\mathrel{\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}}&\neg A\hfil\hskip 14.22636pt&\iota(A{}^{\ominus})&\,\mathrel{\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}}&\neg A\\
\end{array}

Lemma 51

(Intuitionistic Conservativity)
.

If
\Gamma\vdash_{\textup{PRJ}}P

then
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}\iota(P)
.

Proof.

We shall prove a slightly more general property.
We claim that
if
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ}}t:P
,
then
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}\iota(P)
.
Observe that the general property implies the statement of the lemma
taking
\Delta=\varnothing
.

To prove the general property,
recall by Prop.
50

that
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ}}t:P

if and only if

\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t:P

and we proceed by induction on the derivation
of
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}t:P
in
PRJ
\star
.
The more interesting cases are
the axiom rule (
Ax
)
and the positive weak introduction and elimination (
I
{}_{\circ}^{+}
,
E
{}_{\circ}^{+}
).

1.

\textup{{Ax}}\mathtt{J}
:
Let
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}x:P

where
(x:P)\in(\Gamma,\Delta)

and
x\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)
.
Hence
(x:P)\in\Gamma

and
\iota(P)\in\iota(\Gamma)
,
so
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}\iota(P)
by
ax
.

2.

\textup{{Abs}}\mathtt{J}
:
Let
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t\mathrel{{\RHD\!\!\!\LHD_{P}}}s:P

be derived from
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t:A{}^{+}

and
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}s:A{}^{-}
.
Then:

{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 14.16345pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\text{IH }$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.54375pt,depth=-2.08122pt,width=41.68512pt\hbox{\kern 2.77501pt$\textsc{\footnotesize{}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota(\Gamma)\vdash\neg A$}}}}\end{array}\hskip 14.22636pt\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 10.30931pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\text{IH }$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.54375pt,depth=-2.08122pt,width=33.97684pt\hbox{\kern 2.77501pt$\textsc{\footnotesize{}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota(\Gamma)\vdash A$}}}}\end{array}\\
&\,\textup{{{E$\lnot$}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash\bot\end{array}}\\
&\,\textup{{{E$\bot$}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash\iota(P)\end{array}}

3.

\textup{{I${}_{\circ}^{+}$}}\mathtt{J}
:
Let
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}\mathsf{\textup{\Circle}}{}^{+}_{(x:A{}^{\ominus})}.\,t:A{}^{\oplus}

be derived from
\Gamma,\Delta,x:A{}^{\ominus}\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)\cup\{x\}}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t:A{}^{+}
.
By IH we have that
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}A
, exactly as required.

4.

\textup{{I${}_{\circ}^{-}$}}\mathtt{J}
:
Let
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}\mathsf{\textup{\Circle}}{}^{+}_{(x:A{}^{\oplus})}.\,t:A{}^{\ominus}

be derived from
\Gamma,\Delta,x:A{}^{\oplus}\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t:A{}^{-}
.
Then:

{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt\text{IH }\\
&\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),A\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}\neg A\end{array}}\hskip 14.22636pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\\
&\,\textup{{{ax}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),A\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}A\end{array}}\\
&\,\textup{{{E$\lnot$}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),A\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}\bot\end{array}}\\
&\,\textup{{{I$\lnot$}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}\neg A\end{array}}

5.

\textup{{E${}_{\circ}^{+}$}}\mathtt{J}
:
Let
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t\bullet\!\!{}^{+}\,s:A{}^{+}

be derived from
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t:A{}^{\oplus}

and
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash_{\textup{PRK}}s:A{}^{\ominus}
.
By IH on the first premise, we have that
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}A
,
exactly as required.

6.

\textup{{E${}_{\circ}^{-}$}}\mathtt{J}
:
Let
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t\bullet\!\!{}^{-}\,s:A{}^{-}

be derived from
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t:A{}^{\ominus}

and
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}s:A{}^{\oplus}
.
By IH on the first premise, we have that
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}\neg A
,
exactly as required.

7.

\textup{{I${}_{\land}^{+}$}}\mathtt{J}
:
Let
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}\langle t,s\rangle{}^{+}:(A\land B){}^{+}

be derived from
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t:A{}^{\oplus}

and
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}s:B{}^{\oplus}
.
By IH,
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}A

and
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}B
,
which imply
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}A\land B

by
I
\land
.

8.

\textup{{I${}_{\lor}^{-}$}}\mathtt{J}
:
Let
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}\langle t,s\rangle{}^{-}:(A\lor B){}^{-}

be derived from
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t:A{}^{\ominus}

and
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}s:B{}^{\ominus}
.
By IH,
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}\neg A

and
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}\neg B
.
Let
\pi_{1}
be the derivation:

{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt\text{IH }\\
&\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash\neg A\end{array}}\\
&\,\textup{{{W}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),A\lor B,A\vdash\neg A\end{array}}\hskip 14.22636pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\\
&\,\text{{{Ax}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),A\lor B,A\vdash A\end{array}}\\
&\,\textup{{{E$\lnot$}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),A\lor B,A\vdash\bot\end{array}}

and, symmetrically, let
\pi_{2}
be a derivation of

\iota(\Gamma),A\lor B,B\vdash\bot
.
Then:

{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\\
&\,\text{{{Ax}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),A\lor B\vdash A\lor B\end{array}}\hskip 14.22636pt\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\vdots\\
\pi_{1}\end{array}\hskip 14.22636pt\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\vdots\\
\pi_{2}\end{array}\\
&\,\textup{{{E$\lor$}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),A\lor B\vdash\bot\end{array}}\\
&\,\textup{{{I$\lnot$}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash\neg(A\lor B)\end{array}}

9.

\textup{{E${}^{+}_{\land{i}}$}}\mathtt{J}
:
Let
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}\pi_{i}^{+}(t):A_{i}{}^{\oplus}

be derived from
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t:(A_{1}\land A_{2}){}^{+}

for some
i\in\{1,2\}
.
By IH,
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}A_{1}\land A_{2}
,
which implies
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}A_{i}

by
E
\land_{i}
.

10.

\textup{{E${}^{-}_{\lor{i}}$}}\mathtt{J}
:
Let
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}\pi_{i}^{-}(t):A_{i}{}^{\ominus}

be derived from
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t:(A_{1}\lor A_{2}){}^{-}

for some
i\in\{1,2\}
.
By IH,
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}\neg(A_{1}\lor A_{2})
.
Then:

{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt\text{IH }\\
&\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash\neg(A_{1}\lor A_{2})\end{array}}\\
&\,\textup{{{W}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),A_{i}\vdash\neg(A_{1}\lor A_{2})\end{array}}\hskip 14.22636pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\\
&\,\text{{{Ax}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),A_{i}\vdash A_{i}\end{array}}\\
&\,\textup{{{I$\lor_{i}$}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),A_{i}\vdash A_{1}\lor A_{2}\end{array}}\\
&\,\textup{{{E$\lnot$}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),A_{i}\vdash\bot\end{array}}\\
&\,\textup{{{I$\lnot$}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash\neg A_{i}\end{array}}

11.

\textup{{I${}^{+}_{\lor{i}}$}}\mathtt{J}
:
Let
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}\mathsf{in}_{i}\!\!{}^{+}(t):(A_{1}\lor A_{2}){}^{+}

be derived from
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t:A_{i}{}^{\oplus}

for some
i\in\{1,2\}
.
By IH,
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}A_{i}
,
which implies
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}A_{1}\lor A_{2}

by
I
\lor_{i}
.

12.

\textup{{I${}^{-}_{\land{i}}$}}\mathtt{J}
:
Let
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}\mathsf{in}_{i}\!\!{}^{-}(t):(A_{1}\land A_{2}){}^{-}

be derived from
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t:A_{i}{}^{\ominus}

for some
i\in\{1,2\}
.
By IH,
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}\neg A_{i}
. Then:

{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt\text{IH }\\
&\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash\neg A_{i}\end{array}}\\
&\,\textup{{{W}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),A_{1}\land A_{2}\vdash\neg A_{i}\end{array}}\hskip 14.22636pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\\
&\,\text{{{Ax}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),A_{1}\land A_{2}\vdash A_{1}\land A_{2}\end{array}}\\
&\,\textup{{{E$\land_{i}$}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),A_{1}\land A_{2}\vdash A_{i}\end{array}}\\
&\,\textup{{{E$\lnot$}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),A_{1}\land A_{2}\vdash\bot\end{array}}\\
&\,\textup{{{I$\lnot$}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash\neg(A_{1}\land A_{2})\end{array}}

13.

\textup{{E${}_{\lor}^{+}$}}\mathtt{J}
:
Let

\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}\delta{}^{+}t\,[_{x:A{}^{\oplus}}.s][_{y:B{}^{\oplus}}.u]:P

be derived from
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t:(A\lor B){}^{+}

and
\Gamma,\Delta,x:A{}^{\oplus}\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}s:P

and
\Gamma,\Delta,y:B{}^{\oplus}\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}u:P
.
By IH,
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}A\lor B

and
\iota(\Gamma),A\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}\iota(P)

and
\iota(\Gamma),B\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}\iota(P)
,
which imply
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}\iota(P)

by
E
\lor
.

14.

\textup{{I${}_{\rightarrow}^{+}$}}\mathtt{J}
:
Let
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}\lambda{}^{+}_{(x:A{}^{\oplus})}.{t}:(A\rightarrow B){}^{+}

be derived from
\Gamma,\Delta,x:A{}^{\oplus}\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t:B{}^{\oplus}
.
By IH,
\iota(\Gamma),A\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}B
,
which implies
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}A\rightarrow B

by
I
\rightarrow
.

15.

\textup{{I${}_{\ltimes}^{-}$}}\mathtt{J}
:
Let
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}\lambda{}^{-}_{(x:A{}^{\ominus})}.{t}:(A\ltimes B){}^{-}

be derived from
\Gamma,\Delta,x:A{}^{\ominus}\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t:B{}^{\ominus}
.
Let
\pi
be the derivation:

{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt\text{IH }\\
&\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),\neg A\vdash\neg B\end{array}}\\
&\,\textup{{{W}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),A\ltimes B,\neg A\vdash\neg B\end{array}}\hskip 14.22636pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\\
&\,\textup{{{ax}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),A\ltimes B\vdash A\ltimes B\end{array}}\hskip 14.22636pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\\
&\,\text{{{Ax}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),A\ltimes B,\neg A,B\vdash\neg A\end{array}}\\
&\,\textup{{{E$\ltimes$}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),A\ltimes B\vdash\neg A\end{array}}\\
&\,\textup{{{Cut}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),A\ltimes B\vdash\neg B\end{array}}

Then:

{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\vdots\\
\pi\end{array}\hskip 14.22636pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\\
&\,\textup{{{ax}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),A\ltimes B\vdash A\ltimes B\end{array}}\hskip 14.22636pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\\
&\,\textup{{{ax}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),A\ltimes B,\neg A,B\vdash B\end{array}}\\
&\,\textup{{{E$\ltimes$}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),A\ltimes B\vdash B\end{array}}\\
&\,\textup{{{E$\lnot$}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),A\ltimes B\vdash\bot\end{array}}\\
&\,\textup{{{I$\lnot$}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash\neg(A\ltimes B)\end{array}}

16.

\textup{{E${}_{\rightarrow}^{+}$}}\mathtt{J}
:
Let
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t@{}^{+}s:B{}^{\oplus}

be derived from
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t:(A\rightarrow B){}^{+}

and
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}s:A{}^{\oplus}
.
By IH,
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}A\rightarrow B

and
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}A
,
which imply
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}B

by
E
\rightarrow
.

17.

\textup{{E${}_{\ltimes}^{-}$}}\mathtt{J}
:
Let
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t@{}^{-}s:B{}^{\ominus}

be derived from
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t:(A\ltimes B){}^{-}

and
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}s:A{}^{\ominus}
.
Let
\pi
be the derivation:

{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 14.16345pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\text{IH }$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.54375pt,depth=-2.08122pt,width=41.68512pt\hbox{\kern 2.77501pt$\textsc{\footnotesize{}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota(\Gamma)\vdash\neg A$}}}}\end{array}\\
&\,\textup{{{W}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),B\vdash\neg A\end{array}}\hskip 14.22636pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\\
&\,\textup{{{ax}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),B\vdash B\end{array}}\\
&\,\textup{{{I$\ltimes$}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),B\vdash A\ltimes B\end{array}}

Then:

{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 26.3663pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\text{IH }$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.54375pt,depth=-2.08122pt,width=66.09082pt\hbox{\kern 2.77501pt$\textsc{\footnotesize{}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota(\Gamma)\vdash\neg(A\ltimes B)$}}}}\end{array}\\
&\,\textup{{{W}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),B\vdash\neg(A\ltimes B)\end{array}}\hskip 14.22636pt\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\vdots\\
\pi\end{array}\\
&\,\textup{{{E$\lnot$}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),B\vdash\bot\end{array}}\\
&\,\textup{{{I$\lnot$}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash\neg B\end{array}}

18.

\textup{{I${}_{\ltimes}^{+}$}}\mathtt{J}
:
Let
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}(t\,\bm{;}\!{}^{+}s):(A\ltimes B){}^{+}

be derived from
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t:A{}^{\ominus}

and
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}s:B{}^{\oplus}
.
By IH,
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}\neg A

and
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}B
,
which imply
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}A\ltimes B

by
I
\ltimes
.

19.

\textup{{I${}_{\rightarrow}^{-}$}}\mathtt{J}
:
Let
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}(t\,\bm{;}\!{}^{-}s):(A\rightarrow B){}^{-}

be derived from
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t:A{}^{\oplus}

and
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}s:B{}^{\ominus}
.
Let
\pi
be the derivation:

{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\\
&\,\textup{{{ax}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),A\rightarrow B\vdash A\rightarrow B\end{array}}\hskip 14.22636pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt\text{IH }\\
&\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash A\end{array}}\\
&\,\textup{{{W}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),A\rightarrow B\vdash A\end{array}}\\
&\,\textup{{{E$\rightarrow$}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),A\rightarrow B\vdash B\end{array}}

Then:

{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt\text{IH }\\
&\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash\neg B\end{array}}\\
&\,\textup{{{W}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),A\rightarrow B\vdash\neg B\end{array}}\hskip 14.22636pt\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\vdots\\
\pi\end{array}\\
&\,\textup{{{E$\lnot$}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),A\rightarrow B\vdash\bot\end{array}}\\
&\,\textup{{{I$\lnot$}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash\neg(A\rightarrow B)\end{array}}

20.

\textup{{E${}_{\ltimes}^{+}$}}\mathtt{J}
:
Let
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}\varrho^{+}{t}[_{x;y}.{s}]:P

be derived from

\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t:(A\ltimes B){}^{+}

and

\Gamma,\Delta,x:A{}^{\ominus},y:B{}^{\oplus}\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}s:P
.
By IH,
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}A\ltimes B

and
\iota(\Gamma),\neg A,B\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}\iota(P)

which implies
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}\iota(P)

by
E
\ltimes
.

21.

\textup{{I${}_{\lnot}^{+}$}}\mathtt{J}
:
Let
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}\mathsf{N}{}^{+}t:(\neg A){}^{+}

be derived from
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t:A{}^{\ominus}
.
By IH,
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}\neg A
, exactly as required.

22.

\textup{{I${}_{\lnot}^{-}$}}\mathtt{J}
:
Let
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}\mathsf{N}{}^{-}t:(\neg A){}^{-}

be derived from
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t:A{}^{\oplus}
.
By IH,
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}A
. Then:

{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\\
&\,\text{{{Ax}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),\neg A\vdash\neg A\end{array}}\hskip 14.22636pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt\text{IH }\\
&\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash A\end{array}}\\
&\,\textup{{{W}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),\neg A\vdash A\end{array}}\\
&\,\textup{{{E$\lnot$}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),\neg A\vdash\bot\end{array}}\\
&\,\textup{{{I$\lnot$}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash\neg\neg A\end{array}}

23.

\textup{{E${}_{\lnot}^{+}$}}\mathtt{J}
:
Let
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}\mathsf{M}{}^{+}t:A{}^{\ominus}

be derived from
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t:(\neg A){}^{+}
.
By IH,
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}\neg A
, exactly as required.

24.

\textup{{I${}_{\forall}^{+}$}}\mathtt{J}
:
Let

\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}\lambda{}^{+}_{\alpha}.\,t:(\forall\alpha.\,A){}^{+}

be derived from

\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t:A{}^{\oplus}
,
where
\alpha\notin\mathsf{ftv}(\Gamma,\Delta)
.
By IH,
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}A
.
Moreover, note that
\alpha\notin\mathsf{ftv}(\iota(\Gamma))

since
\alpha\notin\mathsf{ftv}(\Gamma)
.
Hence by
I
\forall
we have that
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}\forall\alpha.\,A
.

25.

\textup{{I${}_{\exists}^{-}$}}\mathtt{J}
:
Let

\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}\lambda{}^{-}_{\alpha}.\,t:(\exists\alpha.\,A){}^{-}

be derived from

\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t:A{}^{\ominus}
,
where
\alpha\not\in\mathsf{ftv}(\Gamma)
.
By IH,
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}\neg A
.
Moreover, note that
\alpha\notin\mathsf{ftv}(\iota(\Gamma))
.
Let
\pi
be the derivation:

{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\\
&\,\text{{{Ax}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),\exists\alpha.\,A\vdash\exists\alpha.\,A\end{array}}\hskip 14.22636pt\alpha\notin\mathsf{ftv}(\iota(\Gamma),\exists\alpha.\,A)\\
&\,\textup{{{E$\exists$}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),\exists\alpha.\,A\vdash A\end{array}}

Then:

{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt\text{IH }\\
&\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash\neg A\end{array}}\\
&\,\textup{{{W}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),\exists\alpha.\,A\vdash\neg A\end{array}}\hskip 14.22636pt\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\vdots\\
\pi\end{array}\\
&\,\textup{{{E$\lnot$}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),\exists\alpha.\,A\vdash\bot\end{array}}\\
&\,\textup{{{I$\lnot$}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash\neg\exists\alpha.\,A\end{array}}

26.

\textup{{E${}_{\forall}^{+}$}}\mathtt{J}
:
Let

\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t@{}^{+}A:B{}^{\oplus}\{\alpha:=A\}

be derived from

\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t:(\forall\alpha.\,B){}^{+}
.
By IH,
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}\forall\alpha.\,B
,
which implies

\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}B\{\alpha:=A\}

by
E
\forall
.

27.

\textup{{E${}_{\exists}^{-}$}}\mathtt{J}
:
Let

\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t@{}^{-}A:B{}^{\ominus}\{\alpha:=A\}

be derived from

\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t:(\exists\alpha.\,B){}^{-}
.
By IH,
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}\neg\exists\alpha.\,B
.
Let
\pi
be the derivation:

{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\\
&\,\text{{{Ax}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),B\{\alpha:=A\}\vdash B\{\alpha:=A\}\end{array}}\\
&\,\textup{{{I$\exists$}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),B\{\alpha:=A\}\vdash\exists\alpha.\,B\end{array}}

Then:

{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt\text{IH }\\
&\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash\neg\exists\alpha.\,B\end{array}}\\
&\,\textup{{{W}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),B\{\alpha:=A\}\vdash\neg\exists\alpha.\,B\end{array}}\hskip 14.22636pt\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\vdots\\
\pi\end{array}\\
&\,\textup{{{E$\lnot$}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),B\{\alpha:=A\}\vdash\bot\end{array}}\\
&\,\textup{{{I$\lnot$}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma)\vdash\neg B\{\alpha:=A\}\end{array}}

28.

\textup{{I${}_{\exists}^{+}$}}\mathtt{J}
:
Let

\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}\langle A,t\rangle{}^{+}:(\exists\alpha.\,B){}^{+}

be derived from

\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t:B{}^{\oplus}\{\alpha:=A\}
.
By IH,

\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}B\{\alpha:=A\}
,
which implies

\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}\exists\alpha.\,B

by
I
\exists
.

29.

\textup{{I${}_{\forall}^{-}$}}\mathtt{J}
:
Let

\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}\langle A,t\rangle{}^{-}:(\forall\alpha.\,B){}^{-}

be derived from

\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)}_{\textup{PRJ$\star$}}t:B{}^{\ominus}\{\alpha:=A\}
.
By IH,

\iota(\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{NJ}}\neg B\{\alpha:=A\}
.
Let
\pi
be the derivation:

{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\\
&\,\text{{{Ax}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),\forall\alpha.\,B\vdash\forall\alpha.\,B\end{array}}\\
&\,\textup{{{E$\forall$}}}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
\iota(\Gamma),\forall\alpha.\,B\vdash B\{\alpha:=A\}\end{array}}

Then:

{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt{\small\begin{array}[b]{c@{}r}\hskip-5.69046pt\text{IH }\\
&\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\\
